## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

This is a competent, consistent investor update that follows a clear route: contents (2), highlights and summary (3), financial summary table and narrative (4 to 6), operations, capacity, technology, outlook (7 to 11), then photos, charts and statements as reference. The slide 4 table is well built, with grouped rows, a tinted current-quarter column and a YoY column, and the photo pages (13 to 24) are large and well captioned. The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny. It reads fine as a document sent ahead of an earnings call but would not work projected.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the slide 4 table structure with grouped metric rows, the tinted Q4-2020 column and the YoY column, plus the full-bleed captioned photos on 13 to 24 and the numbered contents on slide 2.

**Most useful repair:** Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**What would change the score:** I would give the bulk of the summary-slide narrative to a few emphasized figures, add titles that state the point of each chart on 8, 10, 25 and 26, and label the axes and units more clearly.

_Confidence medium. 34 of 34 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a letter-style summary of 2020 highlights, covering volume, cash and profitability, followed by a short list of bullet highlights under those headings. It then moves through financial summaries for the quarter and full year, an operational summary of deliveries, production and deployments, and a vehicle capacity section covering the Fremont, Shanghai and Berlin factories. Core technology and other highlights follow, covering Autopilot, FSD, battery and powertrain, energy storage and solar. The outlook slide sets out the company's approach to volume, cash, profit and product for 2021. The back half is mostly reference material: photos of factories and vehicles, key metric charts, full financial statements, a reconciliation of GAAP to non-GAAP measures, and a closing section with forward-looking statements and definitions. Slides 4, 6 and 11 are the most useful to study for how a financial update pairs a summary table with a short narrative and a guidance section.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Tesla Q4 and FY2020 Update deck have?**

The deck has 34 pages in total, as listed on the cover data for the presentation. Slide 2 gives its contents, with sections running from Highlights on slide 3 to Additional Information on slide 33.

**What does the Tesla deck say about its 2021 volume outlook?**

On slide 11, Tesla says that over a multi-year horizon it expects to achieve 50% average annual growth in vehicle deliveries. It also says that in some years it may grow faster, which it expects to be the case in 2021.

**What does the Tesla deck say about energy storage deployments in 2020?**

On slide 10, Tesla says that for the first time its total battery deployments surpassed 3 GWh in a single year, an 83% increase compared to the prior year. The slide attributes this growth mainly to the popularity of Megapack, its utility scale storage product.

**How is the Tesla Q4 and FY2020 Update deck structured?**

Slide 2 lists the contents: Highlights (slide 3), Financial Summary (slide 4), Operational Summary (slide 7), Vehicle Capacity (slide 8), Core Technology (slide 9), Other Highlights (slide 10), and Outlook (slide 11). The back half covers Photos and Charts (slide 12), Key Metrics (slide 25), Financial Statements (slide 27), and Additional Information (slide 33).

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The highlights slide groups its bullets under clear headings such as Cash, Profitability and Operations, so the key numbers can be scanned quickly.
- Review: The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny.
- Suggested repair: Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**Text**

HIGHLIGHTS
SUMMARY
Cash
Operating cash flow less capex (free cash flow) of $2.8B in 2020
$4.9B increase in our cash and cash equivalents in Q4 to $19.4B
Free cash flow $1.9B in Q4
This past year was transformative for Tesla. Despite unforeseen global
challenges, we outpaced many trends seen elsewhere in the industry as
we significantly increased volumes, profitability and cash generation.
For the full year 2020, we achieved an industry-leading¹ 6.3% operating
margin (despite an increase of SBC to $1.7B). Teams across our
organization, including supply chain, manufacturing, logistics and
delivery, rose to the occasion to ensure strong execution.
Profitability
$721M GAAP net income; $2.5B non-GAAP net income in 2020
In addition, we continued to improve our products and make progress
on our long-term roadmap. We ramped Model 3 in China to over 5,000
cars per week and started production of Model Y at Gigafactory
$270M GAAP net income; $903M non-GAAP net income (ex-SBC*) in Q4
Shanghai less than a year after breaking ground on the expansion. We
$575M GAAP operating income; 5.4% operating margin in Q4
also launched and ramped Model Y in Fremont in 2020.
SBC expense increased to $633M in Q4
In Berlin and Austin, we remain on track to start vehicle production this
year with structural batteries leveraging in-house battery cells. Our
engineering team has made significant progress on Full Self Driving
(FSD) software, with a limited release to customers. Finally, we are
excited to ramp the updated Model S and Model X and deliver our first
Tesla Semi by the end of the year.
Operations
Half a million vehicles produced and delivered in 2020
Model Y production at Gigafactory Shanghai started in December 2020
While 2020 was a critical year for Tesla, we believe that 2021 will be
even more important. Thank you for your trust and support and for
Updated Model S and Model X launched in January 2021
being on this journey with us.
*SBC = stock-based compensation
¹ Based on latest available trailing 12-month (Q4 '19 to Q3 '20) operating margin reported by major OEMs.

- text at x 55.2, y 20.3, w 39.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: challenges, we outpaced many trends seen elsewhere in the industry as
- text at x 13.4, y 22.9, w 34.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: $4.9B increase in our cash and cash equivalents in Q4 to $19.4B
- text at x 55.2, y 22.9, w 37.7, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: we significantly increased volumes, profitability and cash generation.
- text at x 13.4, y 28.0, w 14.3, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Free cash flow $1.9B in Q4
- text at x 55.2, y 28.0, w 39.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: For the full year 2020, we achieved an industry-leading 6.3% operating
- text at x 55.2, y 30.6, w 34.4, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: margin (despite an increase of SBC to $1.7B). Teams across our
- text at x 55.2, y 33.2, w 35.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: organization, including supply chain, manufacturing, logistics and
- text at x 55.2, y 35.8, w 30.9, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: delivery, rose to the occasion to ensure strong execution.
- text at x 4.4, y 40.8, w 6.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Profitability
- text at x 4.4, y 40.8, w 6.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Profitability
- text at x 13.4, y 41.0, w 33.9, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: $721M GAAP net income; $2.5B non-GAAP net income in 2020
- text at x 55.2, y 41.0, w 38.3, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: In addition, we continued to improve our products and make progress
- text at x 85.1, y 28.0, w 0.3, h 1.2 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.20; #FFFFFF]: 1
- text at x 55.2, y 43.6, w 38.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: on our long-term roadmap. We ramped Model 3 in China to over 5,000
- text at x 55.2, y 46.1, w 35.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: cars per week and started production of Model Y at Gigafactory

- text at x 55.2, y 48.7, w 38.2, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Shanghai less than a year after breaking ground on the expansion. We

- text at x 55.2, y 51.3, w 30.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: also launched and ramped Model Y in Fremont in 2020.

- text at x 55.2, y 59.1, w 36.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: year with structural batteries leveraging in-house battery cells. Our

### Slide 4

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The quarterly financial summary table places five periods and a year-over-year column side by side, letting the reader compare trends at a glance.
- Review: The slide 4 table is well built, with grouped rows, a tinted current-quarter column and a YoY column, and the photo pages (13 to 24) are large and well captioned.
- Worth keeping: Keep the slide 4 table structure with grouped metric rows, the tinted Q4-2020 column and the YoY column, plus the full-bleed captioned photos on 13 to 24 and the numbered contents on slide 2.

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny.
- Suggested repair: Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**Text**

FINANCIAL SUMMARY
Revenue
Total revenue grew 46% YoY in Q4. This was primarily achieved through substantial growth in vehicle deliveries as well as growth in other
parts of the business. At the same time, vehicle average selling price (ASP) declined by 11% YoY as our product mix continued to shift from
Model S and Model X to the more affordable Model 3 and Model Y.
Profitability
Our operating income improved in Q4 compared to the same period last year to $575M, resulting in a 5.4% operating margin. This
profit level was reached while incurring SBC expense attributable to the 2018 CEO award of $267M in Q4, driven by an increase in our
market capitalization and a new operational milestone becoming probable.
Positive impact from volume growth and regulatory credit revenue growth YoY was mainly offset by lower ASP (including price
reduction of China-made Model 3 and price reductions of Model S and Model X before the introduction of updated models) but also by
a series of notable items. These included a portion of Q4 SBC charges, vehicle warranty accruals, additional supply chain costs, Model
S and Model X changeover costs and other items.
Cash
Quarter-end cash and cash equivalents increased to $19.4B in Q4, driven mainly by our recent capital raise of $5.0B (average price of this
offering was ~$632/share) and free cash flow of $1.9B, partially offset by early debt repayments (early conversion of convertible notes).
6
TESLA

**Layout**

Single white slide with a left-aligned, two-column text arrangement: small row labels sit in a narrow left column starting at about 4.4% of slide width, and body paragraphs sit in a wider right column starting at about 15.9%, each label aligned to the top line of its paragraph. The letter-spaced title FINANCIAL SUMMARY sits top-left at about 9.3% of slide height, with generous empty space below the content. The page number is at bottom-left and a faint wordmark logo is at bottom-right, both near the bottom edge.

**Visuals**

None apart from a small, faint Tesla wordmark logo at bottom-right, drawn as letter-spaced capitals T E S L A in pale grey (about #D9D9D9), with no other photographs, charts, tables or icons on the slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients, shapes, rules or shadows. The title FINANCIAL SUMMARY is set in widely letter-spaced Gotham-style medium sans-serif capitals in dark grey #333333 at about 1.74% of slide height. Row labels (Revenue, Profitability, Cash) are Gotham-style book sans-serif in near-black #111111 at about 1.79% of slide height. Body paragraphs are Gotham-style book sans-serif in mid grey #6E6E6E at about 1.79% of slide height, with generous line spacing of roughly 2.6% of slide height between lines and large gaps between sections. The page number is Gotham-style medium in light grey #BABABA at about 1.58% of slide height. Margins are wide and text is flush left with ragged right edges.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- title at x 4.4, y 9.3, w 16.6, h 1.7 [Gotham SSm Medium, sans-serif, capitals, letter-spacing wide; font size 1.74% of slide height; #333333; left aligned]: FINANCIAL SUMMARY
- body at x 4.4, y 18.7, w 4.7, h 1.8 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #111111; left aligned]: Revenue (Row label for the first section.)
- body at x 15.9, y 18.7, w 74.7, h 6.6 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #6E6E6E; left aligned]: Total revenue grew 46% YoY in Q4. This was primarily achieved through substantial growth in vehicle deliveries as well as growth in other / parts of the business. At the same time, vehicle average selling price (ASP) declined by 11% YoY as our product mix continued to shift from / Model S and Model X to the more affordable Model 3 and Model Y. (Three lines with line pitch of about 2.6% of slide height.)
- body at x 4.4, y 34.2, w 6.6, h 1.8 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #111111; left aligned]: Profitability (Row label for the second section, top-aligned with its first paragraph.)
- body at x 15.9, y 34.2, w 72.1, h 6.6 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #6E6E6E; left aligned]: Our operating income improved in Q4 compared to the same period last year to $575M, resulting in a 5.4% operating margin. This / profit level was reached while incurring SBC expense attributable to the 2018 CEO award of $267M in Q4, driven by an increase in our / market capitalization and a new operational milestone becoming probable. (First paragraph of the Profitability section, three lines.)
- body at x 15.9, y 44.6, w 72.9, h 8.5 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #6E6E6E; left aligned]: Positive impact from volume growth and regulatory credit revenue growth YoY was mainly offset by lower ASP (including price / reduction of China-made Model 3 and price reductions of Model S and Model X before the introduction of updated models) but also by / a series of notable items. These included a portion of Q4 SBC charges, vehicle warranty accruals, additional supply chain costs, Model / S and Model X changeover costs and other items. (Second paragraph of the Profitability section, four lines, separated from the first paragraph by one blank line of about 2.6% of slide height.)
- body at x 4.4, y 62.7, w 2.7, h 1.8 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #111111; left aligned]: Cash (Row label for the third section.)
- body at x 15.9, y 62.7, w 74.1, h 4.2 [Gotham SSm Book, sans-serif, regular weight; font size 1.79% of slide height; #6E6E6E; left aligned]: Quarter-end cash and cash equivalents increased to $19.4B in Q4, driven mainly by our recent capital raise of $5.0B (average price of this / offering was ~$632/share) and free cash flow of $1.9B, partially offset by early debt repayments (early conversion of convertible notes). (Two lines.)

### Slide 7

**Text**

OPERATIONAL SUMMARY / (Unaudited) / Q4-2019 / Q1-2020 / Q2-2020 / Q3-2020 / Q4-2020 / YoY / Model S/X production / 17,933 / 15,390 / 6,326 / 16,992 / 16,097 / -10% / Model 3/Y production / 86,958 / 87,282 / 75,946 / 128,044 / 163,660 / 88% / Total production / 104,891 / 102,672 / 82,272 / 145,036 / 179,757 / 71% / Model S/X deliveries / 19,475 / 12,230 / 10,614 / 15,275 / 18,966 / -3% / Model 3/Y deliveries / 92,620 / 76,266 / 80,277 / 124,318 / 161,701 / 75% / Total deliveries / 112,095 / 88,496 / 90,891 / 139,593 / 180,667 / 61% / of which subject to operating lease accounting / 8,848 / 6,104 / 4,716 / 10,014 / 13,636 / 54% / Total end of quarter operating lease vehicle count / 49,901 / 53,159 / 54,519 / 61,638 / 72,089 / 44% / Global vehicle inventory (days of supply)(1) / 10 / 25 / 17 / 14 / 11 / 10% / Solar deployed (MW) / 54 / 35 / 27 / 57 / 86 / 59% / Storage deployed (MWh) / 530 / 260 / 419 / 759 / 1,584 / 199% / Store and service locations / 433 / 438 / 446 / 466 / 523 / 21% / Mobile service fleet / 743 / 756 / 769 / 780 / 823 / 11% / Supercharger stations / 1,821 / 1,917 / 2,035 / 2,181 / 2,564 / 41% / Supercharger connectors / 16,104 / 17,007 / 18,100 / 19,437 / 23,277 / 45% / (1) Days of supply is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the quarter's deliveries and using 75 trading days (aligned with Automotive News definition). / 7

**Layout**

Single full-width data table on a white page. A small uppercase letterspaced title block sits top-left at about 4.4% from the left edge, with the subtitle '(Unaudited)' beneath it. The table starts at about 13.7% from the top and spans from 4.4% to about 93.3% horizontally, with a label column on the left and five quarter columns plus a YoY column on the right. The footnote sits near the bottom left at about 88% height, the page number at bottom left at about 95% height, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom right. No footer band or rule.

**Visuals**

A single data table, not a chart. Columns: row label (unnamed), Q4-2019, Q1-2020, Q2-2020, Q3-2020, Q4-2020, YoY. Header row is dark grey #7F7F7F bar with white text. Rows, values as printed: Model S/X production 17,933 / 15,390 / 6,326 / 16,992 / 16,097 / -10%; Model 3/Y production 86,958 / 87,282 / 75,946 / 128,044 / 163,660 / 88%; Total production (bold) 104,891 / 102,672 / 82,272 / 145,036 / 179,757 / 71%; blank spacer row; Model S/X deliveries 19,475 / 12,230 / 10,614 / 15,275 / 18,966 / -3%; Model 3/Y deliveries 92,620 / 76,266 / 80,277 / 124,318 / 161,701 / 75%; Total deliveries (bold) 112,095 / 88,496 / 90,891 / 139,593 / 180,667 / 61%; indented 'of which subject to operating lease accounting' 8,848 / 6,104 / 4,716 / 10,014 / 13,636 / 54%; blank spacer; Total end of quarter operating lease vehicle count 49,901 / 53,159 / 54,519 / 61,638 / 72,089 / 44%; Global vehicle inventory (days of supply)(1) 10 / 25 / 17 / 14 / 11 / 10%; blank spacer; Solar deployed (MW) 54 / 35 / 27 / 57 / 86 / 59%; Storage deployed (MWh) 530 / 260 / 419 / 759 / 1,584 / 199%; blank spacer; Store and service locations 433 / 438 / 446 / 466 / 523 / 21%; Mobile service fleet 743 / 756 / 769 / 780 / 823 / 11%; blank spacer; Supercharger stations 1,821 / 1,917 / 2,035 / 2,181 / 2,564 / 41%; Supercharger connectors 16,104 / 17,007 / 18,100 / 19,437 / 23,277 / 45%. Highlight: the Q4-2020 column data cells carry a pale pink tint (about #F2DCDB) over the grey banding. No bars, lines or charts. Row banding alternates light grey #F2F2F2 with white. No photographs, icons or logos besides the faint wordmark.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate data slide. Title in dark grey #333333, uppercase, wide letterspacing, Gotham-style geometric sans-serif, medium weight, about 1.7% of slide height. Subtitle in the same style, medium weight. Table labels and values in black #000000, Gotham Book-style geometric sans-serif, regular weight, about 1.8% of slide height (roughly 1.8% body text, so body reads slightly larger than the title cap height). Table header text white on #7F7F7F. Row bands #F2F2F2 with white gutters, no borders, square corners, no shadows, no gradients. Q4-2020 column tinted pale pink #F2DCDB. Footnote in very light grey #BFBFBF, about 1.2% of slide height. Page number in light grey #BABABA. Wordmark in very pale grey, widely letterspaced capitals.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The capacity table pairs each factory with its installed capacity and status, and the explanatory note separates capacity from production rate.

**Text**

VEHICLE CAPACITY
Fremont
Over the past few weeks, we have been upgrading our Fremont Factory to launch the new Model S and Model X. These changes include a new powertrain (battery modules, battery packs, drive units), an entirely new interior, exterior updates and other improvements. Production will resume in Q1 and ramp back to full capacity over time. We also continue to increase Model Y production, including integration of the single-piece rear underbody castings, to meet customer demand.
Shanghai
Gigafactory Shanghai has demonstrated the ability to sustain Model 3 production at or above a run rate of 250,000/year. Model Y production started in late 2020 and is in the process of ramping to full capacity. Customer response to both Model 3 and Model Y continues to be strong. We recently started shipping Model 3 vehicles from Gigafactory Shanghai to several countries in Europe and APAC, which supplements production from the Fremont Factory for those markets.
Berlin-Brandenburg
Local production and deliveries remain a key part of our growth strategy. While our total market share in Europe increased in 2020, Gigafactory Berlin should enable a significant increase in local deliveries, similar to what we saw after constructing Gigafactory Shanghai. Buildout of our Berlin factory continues as planned, and we have already started to move machinery into the building.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white slide. The section label VEHICLE CAPACITY sits top-left at about 9% of the slide height, with the left text column (x 4.4% to 50%) and the right column (x 54% to 96%) beneath it, both starting near 18.7% height. The left column holds three stacked headed paragraphs separated by thin horizontal rules; the right column holds a grey-headed table, a small grey note, a line chart, its title and a source line. Left margin is about 4.4% of width; the page number sits bottom-left at about 95% height and the faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Table: titled Installed Annual Capacity with columns Installed Annual Capacity (merged header with the first two columns), Current and Status. Rows: Fremont | Model S / Model X | 100,000 | Production; (blank) | Model 3 / Model Y | 500,000 | Production; Shanghai | Model 3 / Model Y | 450,000 | Production; Berlin | Model Y | - | Construction; Texas | Model Y | - | Construction; (blank) | Cybertruck | - | In development; TBD | Tesla Semi | - | In development; (blank) | Roadster | - | In development; (blank) | Future Product | - | In development.
Chart: line chart titled with no in-chart title, placed below the table. Y axis is percent of global market share, labelled 0.0%, 0.5%, 1.0% and 1.5% with light horizontal gridlines; range 0.0% to 1.5%. X axis is years 2012 to 2020, one label per year. Legend at top-left of plot: US / Canada (blue line), Europe (red line), China (green line). Values are not printed; estimates: US / Canada about 0.02% (2012), 0.11% (2013), 0.10% (2014), 0.14% (2015), 0.24% (2016), 0.26% (2017), 1.02% (2018), 1.04% (2019), 1.38% (2020). Europe about 0.00% (2012), 0.04% (2013), 0.08% (2014), 0.12% (2015), 0.11% (2016), 0.19% (2017), 0.20% (2018), 0.70% (2019), 0.85% (2020). China about 0.00% (2012), 0.00% (2013), 0.02% (2014), 0.02% (2015), 0.05% (2016), 0.08% (2017), 0.06% (2018), 0.22% (2019), 0.73% (2020). No highlighted points. Chart x-axis caption below: Global market share of Tesla vehicles by region.
No photographs or illustrations. Logo: faint TESLA wordmark bottom-right in light grey.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate minimal style. Background white #FFFFFF. Section label in widely letter-spaced capitals, dark grey #333333. Subheadings Fremont, Shanghai and Berlin-Brandenburg in near-black #111111, medium-weight. Body text in mid grey #6E6E6E. Table header fill #7F7F7F with white text #FFFFFF; body cells fill #F2F2F2 with black #000000 text. Table note and chart source in light grey #BFBFBF. Axis labels #595959. Chart series colours: US / Canada blue #4F81BD, Europe red #C0504D, China green #9BBB59. Chart caption #7F7F7F. Typeface: Gotham SSm (Book and Medium weights), a geometric sans-serif. Section label about 1.7% of slide height, body text about 1.8% of slide height, table and chart labels about 1.35%. Thin light grey #D9D9D9 horizontal rules separate left paragraphs. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny.
- Suggested repair: Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**Text**

CORE TECHNOLOGY
Autopilot & Full Self Driving (FSD)
Over the last few months, we released multiple software updates to our FSD City Streets beta testers.1 With each iteration, the system is becoming more robust, resulting in the widening of our user base. We continue to work on the development of our Dojo supercomputer. This computer is designed to process video data from our fleet and train our neural network at an extremely fast rate.
Vehicle Software
As has been the tradition in recent years, we released a holiday software update for our vehicles in December. This time, we included a variety of new games, enabled drivers to make an entrance with custom horn sounds through the external speaker and improved driving visualization among many other updates.
Battery & Powertrain
While our Model S and Model X battery module architecture evolved over the past 8 years, both the battery pack and modules have now been fully redesigned.  Additionally, we have incorporated Model 3 and Model Y motor technology throughout as well as our heat pump for better winter range. These changes enable 5x more high-speed quarter-mile runs than the prior architecture, while further improving energy efficiency.
The Performance versions of Model S and Model X were replaced by Plaid, featuring a tri-motor powertrain with a unique high-speed, high-power rotor. Model S Plaid is the fastest accelerating production car ever made with a 0-60 mph time of <2.0 seconds and a quarter mile in under 9.3 seconds, faster than a Bugatti Chiron.
Sources: Tesla; OEM data

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: the left text column runs from about 4% to 52% of slide width and the right column (chart above table) from about 54% to 96%. The section label CORE TECHNOLOGY sits top-left at about 9% from the top, with the section heads and body paragraphs stacked down the left column and separated by thin horizontal rules. The page number 9 and the footnote sit bottom-left at about 95% from the top, with the sources line and a faint TESLA wordmark bottom-right; left margin about 4.4%, right margin about 4%.

**Visuals**

Bar chart (clustered columns), top right. Title: none printed inside chart; caption below legend reads 'EPA est. range gap continues to widen'. Axes: vertical axis 0 to 450, gridlines every 50, no unit printed; horizontal axis categories Jan-2019, Jan-2020, Jan-2021. Series 1 'Tesla Model S (AWD)' in dark red, series 2 'Highest range non-Tesla EV (AWD)' in steel blue. No data values are printed; estimates: Jan-2019 red about 335, blue about 235; Jan-2020 red about 373, blue about 254; Jan-2021 red about 412, blue about 268. Legend below the axis with small square swatches, red then blue. No highlighted points. Table below the chart: columns Model, 0-60 mph, 1/4 mile; 10 data rows as listed in elements; first row Tesla Model S Plaid is highlighted with a pale pink fill and values <2.0 sec and <9.3 sec. Caption under table reads 'The fastest accelerating production cars ever made'. No photographs or icons; one faint TESLA wordmark bottom-right.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background #FFFFFF with no gradients, shadows or imagery. Section label 'CORE TECHNOLOGY' in GothamSSm-Medium, widely letter-spaced capitals, colour #333333, about 1.74% of slide height. Section heads in GothamSSm-Book, colour #111111, about 1.79%. Body paragraphs GothamSSm-Book, colour #6E6E6E, about 1.79% of slide height, generous line spacing (about 2.5% between baselines). Chart axis numbers and legend in GothamSSm-Book #595959 about 1.35%; chart caption in GothamSSm-Medium #7F7F7F about 1.79%. Sources lines and footnote in GothamSSm-Book #BFBFBF about 1.46%. Table text GothamSSm-Book #000000 about 1.46%, header 'Model' row about 1.58%. Thin light grey horizontal rules (#E0E0E0, about 0.1% stroke) separate text sections. Bars red #B03A3A with a subtle vertical gradient to darker red at the base and blue #3D6FA8 with the same treatment; gridlines #DDDDDD hairline. Table highlight row pale pink #F2DCDB, no other fills, no cell borders except a faint vertical divider.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny.
- Suggested repair: Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**Text**

OTHER HIGHLIGHTS
Energy Storage
Energy storage deployments grew substantially from 2019 to 2020. For the first time, our total battery deployments surpassed 3 GWh in a single year, which is an 83% increase compared to the prior year. This growth was driven mainly by the popularity of Megapack, our utility scale storage product. Powerwall demand continues to increase as the residential business continues to grow. While we have made progress on production, we should see even further increases in supply in the next few months.
Our energy storage business continues to be supply-constrained as backlog remains strong. We are looking to increase capacity both on the manufacturing equipment side as well as supply chain side, to allow us to continue to grow at a similar pace again in 2021.
Solar Retrofit and Solar Roof
In 2020, solar deployments increased to 205 MW, 18% more than the prior year. This growth is the result of meaningful improvements to our solar retrofit strategy, including product simplification, cost reduction and industry-leading pricing. We have also made great progress growing our Solar Roof deployments, as we have expanded the team while simultaneously improving our installation efficiency.

**Layout**

Single-column text slide on a white field. The small letter-spaced section label sits top-left at about 4.4% from the left edge and 9.3% from the top, with the Energy Storage heading, two body paragraphs, a thin horizontal rule, the Solar Retrofit heading and a paragraph stacked down the left column; the text column runs to about 92% of the width. A full-width vertical bar chart occupies the lower part of the slide from about 63% to 92% of the height, with its plot area spanning about 4.5% to 93% of the width. The chart x-axis title is centred below the bars. The page number 10 sits bottom-left at about 2.9% from the left and 95% from the top, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right near 90% of the width at about 94.5% from the top. Margins are about 4.5% left and 9% top.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical bar chart, six bars, one per year 2015 to 2020. Title: none printed above the chart; the x-axis title reads Tesla energy storage deployments in GWh. Y axis: 0.0 to 3.0, gridlines and tick labels every 0.5, no unit printed on the axis. X axis: categories 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020. Series: one series, energy storage deployments in GWh. No values are printed on the bars; estimated values: 2015 about 0.02 (a barely visible sliver), 2016 about 0.2, 2017 about 0.37, 2018 about 1.05, 2019 about 1.67, 2020 about 3.0 (bar reaches the top gridline). The 2020 value is the top of the axis range. Bars are filled with a dark red radial gradient, darker red edges (#8E2A2A) and lighter red centres (#B83C3C), with no outlines. Gridlines are thin light grey (#E0E0E0) horizontal lines at each 0.5 step with the baseline at 0.0 slightly darker. No legend and no highlighted points. Bar widths are equal, about 4.6% of the slide width each. Bar heights in % of slide height: 2016 1.5, 2017 2.7, 2018 7.7, 2019 12.2, 2020 22.5. Tesla wordmark: small letter-spaced grey logo at bottom right, light grey (#BABABA), about 4.5% wide.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, texture or imagery. Section label in GothamSSm-Medium, all capitals with wide letter-spacing, dark grey #333333. Sub-headings in GothamSSm-Book in near-black #111111, roughly 1.8% of slide height. Accent colour is the red of the bars (#A83838 range). A single thin horizontal rule in light grey (#D9D9D9), about 0.1% of height thick, spans from about 4.4% to 93% of the width at about 44% from the top. Bars have a soft radial shading, no corner radii, no shadows.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The outlook slide uses short labelled rows for Volume, Cash, Profit and Product, which keeps forward guidance concise and easy to find.
- Review: The weaknesses are consequential: slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 are small-type text blocks with no takeaway headings or visual emphasis, slide 9 packs three topics, a chart and a table into one page, and the statement pages 28 to 33 are tiny.
- Suggested repair: Break up the dense prose on slides 3, 6, 9, 10 and 11 with a headline takeaway per section and larger key numbers, and split slide 9 into separate pages for Autopilot, battery and the acceleration comparison.

**Text**

OUTLOOK / Introduction / Given the number of significant projects in the pipeline, we have simplified our approach to guidance for 2021, enabling our / teams to remain focused on achieving our long-term goals. / Volume / We are planning to grow our manufacturing capacity as quickly as possible. Over a multi-year horizon, we expect to achieve / 50% average annual growth in vehicle deliveries. In some years we may grow faster, which we expect to be the case in 2021. / The rate of growth will depend on our equipment capacity, operational efficiency and capacity and stability of the supply chain. / Cash / We have sufficient liquidity to fund our product roadmap, long-term capacity expansion plans and other expenses. / Profit / We expect our operating margin will continue to grow over time, continuing to reach industry-leading levels with capacity / expansion and localization plans underway. / Product / We are currently building Model Y capacity at Gigafactory Berlin and Gigafactory Texas and remain on track to start deliveries / from each location in 2021. Gigafactory Shanghai will continue to expand further through the course of the year. Tesla Semi / deliveries will also begin in 2021. / 11 / TESLA

**Layout**

Single-column text index on a white field: a small letter-spaced section label OUTLOOK sits top-left at about 9.3% of slide height, with a two-column arrangement below it in which short bold-style row labels (Introduction, Volume, Cash, Profit, Product) form a narrow left column starting at x 4.4% and the body paragraphs form a wide right column starting at x 20.8%, each paragraph top-aligned with its label. Left margin is about 4.4%, the body column ends near x 88%, and there is no title bar, no header rule and no footer rule. The page number 11 sits bottom-left at about x 2.9%, y 95.2%, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no decoration. Section label OUTLOOK in GothamSSm-Medium, widely letter-spaced capitals, colour #333333. Row labels in GothamSSm-Book, colour #111111, roughly 1.79% of slide height. Body paragraphs in GothamSSm-Book, colour #6E6E6E, roughly 1.79% of slide height, with about 2.6% line pitch. ### Slide 13

**Text**

TESLA MODEL S - FRONT INTERIOR

Tesla logo (faint, bottom right)

**Layout**

Single full-width photograph inside 4.4% side margins, spanning y 12.1% to 92.9%. The title sits top-left at y 9.3% on one baseline, left-aligned to the image edge at x 4.4%. **Visuals**

Photograph (1921 by 960 px), full-width boxed rectangle with square corners, not cropped by any overlay. Subject: interior of a Tesla Model S from the driver's seat looking forward through the windshield. A yoke-style steering wheel with a black rim and grey centre hub sits centre-left, with the 'TESLA' logo on the hub. The instrument cluster shows a small red Tesla car on a dark display. The centre touchscreen shows a games library: a banner for The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt with the character Geralt, and a row of seven game cover thumbnails (Witcher, Cat Quest, Fallout-style, Hotline-style, Stardew-style, and others). Above the windshield is a frameless rear-view mirror. Through the windshield is a hazy blue sky over pale mountain ranges. Red car bodies are visible at the left and right side windows. Lighting is soft, diffuse daylight from the windshield. Dominant colours: white and pale grey (#E9ECEF), walnut wood (#5A3A22), black (#1A1A1A), pale sky blue (#C9DCEB). Composition is centred and symmetrical, with the camera at eye level and a wide framing. Nothing is cropped at the edges except the side window edges.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background (#FFFFFF). Title text is widely letter-spaced uppercase in a geometric sans-serif (Gotham Medium style), with 'TESLA MODEL S -' in dark grey #333333 and 'FRONT INTERIOR' in mid grey #888888, both at about 1.74% of slide height. The page number is light grey #BABABA, about 1.58% of slide height, in the same family. The Tesla wordmark is a very light grey #BABABA logo. No shapes, borders, shadows or gradients; the photograph has square corners and no frame.

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, covering the full slide. Plain white slide with no ornament. **Layout**

Three equal-width column charts sit side by side in a horizontal row across the middle of a white slide, with a title block at the top left and a page number at the bottom left and a faint wordmark at the bottom right. The title is left-aligned at the top-left margin (x 4.4%) and its two lines stack below each other. The left chart's left edge aligns with the title margin at x 4.4%, the right chart's right edge sits near x 96%, and each chart has a bold-free axis title centred beneath it; the middle chart's legend sits below its axis labels at left. Page number '26' sits at the bottom-left margin and the wordmark at the bottom right, both in light grey.

**Visuals**

Three bar charts, all vertical columns with year-quarter category labels rotated 90 degrees under the horizontal axis, and value axes with horizontal light grey gridlines.
Chart 1: Vehicle Deliveries (units), single series of blue bars. Y axis 0 to 500,000 in steps of 50,000 with thousands separators. Values are not printed; about 1Q-2018 110,000; 2Q-2018 127,000; 3Q-2018 185,000; 4Q-2018 245,000; 1Q-2019 280,000; 2Q-2019 333,000; 3Q-2019 347,000; 4Q-2019 368,000; 1Q-2020 393,000; 2Q-2020 389,000; 3Q-2020 432,000; 4Q-2020 500,000. No legend. The 4Q-2020 bar reaches the top of the axis and is the highest.
Chart 2: unlabelled title, with legend below axis labels: blue square 'Operating Cash Flow ($B)' and red square 'Free Cash Flow ($B)'. Y axis -6.0 to 6.0 in steps of 1.0. Each quarter has a blue bar (operating cash flow) and a red bar (free cash flow) side by side, both starting from zero. Values are not printed; about OCF: 1Q-2018 -0.4; 2Q-2018 -0.3; 3Q-2018 1.4; 4Q-2018 2.1; 1Q-2019 1.9; 2Q-2019 2.85; 3Q-2019 2.2; 4Q-2019 2.4; 1Q-2020 2.6; 2Q-2020 2.7; 3Q-2020 4.35; 4Q-2020 5.95. About FCF: 1Q-2018 -3.9; 2Q-2018 -3.5; 3Q-2018 -1.2; 4Q-2018 -0.05; 1Q-2019 0.1; 2Q-2019 1.5; 3Q-2019 1.0; 4Q-2019 1.1; 1Q-2020 1.1; 2Q-2020 0.9; 3Q-2020 1.9; 4Q-2020 2.8. No highlighted points; the 4Q-2020 bars are the tallest.
Chart 3: Net Income ($B), single series of blue bars. Y axis -3.0 to 3.0 in steps of 1.0. Values are not printed; about 1Q-2018 -2.35; 2Q-2018 -2.7; 3Q-2018 -1.8; 4Q-2018 -0.98; 1Q-2019 -0.98; 2Q-2019 -0.65; 3Q-2019 -0.83; 4Q-2019 -0.87; 1Q-2020 -0.15; 2Q-2020 0.36; 3Q-2020 0.56; 4Q-2020 0.72. No legend. Bars hang from the zero line; 2Q-2018 is the most negative.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate-style slide with no decoration. Background white #FFFFFF. Title 'KEY METRICS' in charcoal #333333 and 'TRAILING 12 MONTHS (TTM)' in mid grey #888888, both in a Gotham-style geometric sans-serif, medium weight, all capitals with wide letter-spacing, about 1.7% of slide height (cap height about 1.2%). The '(Unaudited)' subtitle is mid grey #888888, Gotham Medium, about 1.7% of slide height, mixed case. Axis labels and body text are Gotham Book, regular weight, about 1.8% of slide height, colour #595959. Chart axis titles and legend text are Gotham Book in #7F7F7F. Bars use a vertical gradient from dark navy #1F3A5F at the top through steel blue #2F5B8A to lighter blue #4A7DB8 for positive and blue series, and for red bars a gradient from dark red #6E1A1A to brick red #B03A3A. Gridlines are thin light grey #D9D9D9 hairlines. No shapes, borders, shadows or rounded corners. Page number in light grey #BABABA, Gotham Medium, about 1.6% of slide height.

**Layout**

The title and '(Unaudited)' subtitle sit flush left at about 4.4% from the left edge, the table's first column starts at the same margin, and the five numeric columns are right-aligned across the width to about 97% of the slide. The footer runs along the bottom with the page number at far left, the footnote beside it, and a faint TESLA wordmark at bottom right.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with a minimalist corporate financial-table look. Heading 'STATEMENT OF OPERATIONS' is in a geometric grotesque (Gotham SSm Medium) in dark grey #333333, widely letter-spaced, with '(Unaudited)' below it in the same family and colour. Table body is Gotham SSm Book in black #000000, with bold weight only for Gross profit, INCOME FROM OPERATIONS, and NET INCOME ATTRIBUTABLE TO COMMON STOCKHOLDERS. Title text is about 1.7% of slide height and body and table text about 1.5%. Section band rows use light grey #F2F2F2 fills and thin light-grey hairline rules separate rows. The Q4-2020 column carries a pale rose tint around #F2DCDB. Footer page number is Gotham SSm Medium in #BABABA, footnote in Gotham SSm Book #BFBFBF, and the TESLA wordmark in light grey #BFBFBF. **Layout**

A single full-width financial table with the title block 'BALANCE SHEET (Unaudited)' top-left at about 9 to 13 percent of slide height and a left margin of about 4.4 percent. **Design**

The main table runs from about 15% to 68% of slide height with its label column at left and five value columns right-aligned toward the right margin at about 96%. ### Slide 33

**Text**

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
WEBCAST INFORMATION
Tesla will provide a live webcast of its fourth quarter 2020 financial results conference call beginning at 3:30 p.m. PT on January 27, 2021 at ir.tesla.com. This webcast will also be available for replay for approximately one year
thereafter.
CERTAIN TERMS
When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product
deployment volume includes both customer units installed and equipment sales; we report installations at time of commissioning for storage projects or inspection for solar projects, and equipment sales at time of delivery.
"Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based
compensation expense, which is the same measurement for this term pursuant to the performance-based stock option award granted to our CEO in 2018. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures.
NON-GAAP FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP automotive
gross margin, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for
GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to
Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because
management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our
investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business, and allowing
investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported
under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
Certain statements in this update, including statements in the “Outlook” section; statements relating to the future development, production capacity and output rates, demand and market growth, deliveries, deployment, range
and other features and improvements and timing of existing and future Tesla products and technologies such as Model 3, Model Y, Model S, Model X, Tesla Semi, Autopilot and Full Self Driving software and hardware, our energy
storage products, the battery cells we are developing and our manufacturing technologies; statements regarding operating margin, spending and liquidity; and statements regarding construction, expansion, improvements and/or
ramp and related timing at the Tesla Factory, Gigafactory Shanghai, Gigafactory Berlin and Gigafactory Texas are “forward-looking statements” that are subject to risks and uncertainties. These forward-looking statements are
based on management’s current expectations, and, as a result of certain risks and uncertainties, actual results may differ materially from those projected. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual
results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: uncertainties in future macroeconomic and regulatory conditions arising from the current global pandemic; the risk of delays in launching and
manufacturing our products and features cost-effectively; our ability to grow our sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; consumers’ demand for electric vehicles
generally and our vehicles specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with
lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at Gigafactory Nevada; our ability to build and ramp Gigafactory Shanghai, Gigafactory Berlin and Gigafactory Texas in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply
of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international expansion; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition
in the automotive and energy product markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the
status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel and ramp our installation teams; our ability to maintain the
security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our
indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange
Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our quarterly report on Form 10-Q filed with the SEC on October 26, 2020. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update
information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events, or otherwise.
33
TESLA

### Slide 34

**Text**

T E S L A

**Layout**

Single centred wordmark on an otherwise empty dark slide; no title, footer or slide number. The wordmark sits at the horizontal and vertical centre of the slide with wide margins on all sides.

**Visuals**

Tesla wordmark logo, letters T, E, S, L, A spaced evenly on one horizontal line. Each letter is drawn in a flat, squared, geometric style in mid-grey. The logo is small, occupying roughly 44% to 56% of slide width and about 49% to 51% of slide height. No photograph, chart or other graphic.

**Design**

Background: Solid near-black #111111. Solid near-black background #111111 with no gradient, texture or pattern. Logo in mid-grey approximately #8A8A8A. No titles, body text, accents, shapes, lines or shadows.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)
